# Phase 4 — the game: decisions, step map, measurements and port choices (started 2026-10-27)

Step ids below; finished results, finished notes and the build / fix logs are in `docs/PHASE4_HISTORY.md`. Status and open items: the main roadmap (`roadmap-store/ROADMAP.html`; update with `roadmap_store.py write`, see `docs/ROADMAP_PROTOCOL.md`). Scope: single player, **Windows only** (Gabriel, 2026-10-27); multiplayer out of scope (Gabriel, 2026-10-29). Compilers: **MSVC + clang**.

## Status

Status, open work, the flowchart and build results live only on the main roadmap (`roadmap-store/ROADMAP.html`, `docs/ROADMAP_PROTOCOL.md`; build records are on it too). The "At a glance" table that used to be here was moved verbatim to `docs/PHASE4_HISTORY.md` on 2026-11-06 (it was a second copy of the roadmap). This file keeps the decisions, the step map (what each step is and where its code is), measurements and port choices.

## Decisions

Moved to [`DECISIONS.md`](DECISIONS.md) on 2026-11-06 (the Decisions tables, "Camera and controls", "Graphics and display" and "G0 - simulation foundation").

The Status column is the state when the row was last written; the roadmap is authoritative.

Contrast pair rule 0x72b6a0: nothing counts when P <= 0, a pair counts only with w > 0 and R > 0 (a filled entry no longer vetoes Conquer_Pirate). Per tick: global/mode frame, synced RNG, captured session seed (not forced), all sync-store objects as deltas + keyframes, planet data-pack fields, player credits (heuristic vector), fiscal-cycle fields; `ref_rng`/`ref_objects` StateHasher digests (checked against engine/core). `q2trace.py` validate / `to-q1` (ALAMO_CRC 1) / compare. Runs: GC Heart of the Maw (Empire, 10 cycles, hands-off), same + optional Lua probe (credits, owners, `EvaluatePerception` for GALACTIC_ECONOMY §6.4), space skirmish 5400 ticks (human idle vs AI; FoC has no AI-vs-AI slot); `-Mod tr` for Thrawn's Revenge. Unattended mode (`-NonInteractive`, JSON status, exit codes, timeouts, game closed by the reader, cleanup always); menus still need UI driving. selftest.cmd passes (fake game end to end, cleanup twice on scratch folders). **First trace findings:** a GC started from the menu has seed slot 0 and synced RNG 0 at frame 0 (deterministic from 0; the galaxy build + tick 1 = 130 draws). Single player never computes the frame CRC (+0x2C8 always 0, FS ring unwritten). Type +0x11C = zlib CRC-32 of the upper-case name (99/99). 4% ticks missed under load, but every RNG gap was resolved. No credits: the v1 player-vector heuristic failed. Reader v2 adds std::vector/indirect search, a PlayerList dump, light samples and priority. Open: non-UI scenario start (not needed any more: the menu driver), income-breakdown list layout, why the Lua probe writes nothing. **Parity compare (2026-10-30, q2-compare):** `q2trace.py compare <run dir> --port-exe alamo_headless.exe` (logic `analysis/q2_parity.py`) runs the port on the same scenario (`--economy --galactic-ai --campaign <trace campaign> --human <its faction> --seed 0 --ticks <trace ticks>`) and writes `<run dir>/parity/parity_report.txt` + `parity.json`: per metric match / diverges + first tick + module. Port RNG draws per tick are recovered from the CRC log `rng` digest, so no engine change is needed. Vanilla HotM results (port build-msvc 2026-10-29): **fiscal cycles agree** with the port rule (first payment on frame 0, then every 1350; the [L] in engine/galactic campaign.hpp can become [H]). **Divergences:** (1) RNG draws from tick 1: galaxy build + tick 1 = 130 in the original, 59 in the port; 4,527 / 12,906 ticks equal; total 11,355 vs 13,069 (engine/galactic setup draws + engine/ai draw order). (2) Production from tick 56: the original Rebel gets 37 unit batches (first: 25 X-Wing, 3 Y-Wing, 2 Nebulon-B, corvette, gunboat at Yavin on tick 56), the Underworld 17 (first Interceptor IV at The Maw, tick 504); the port Rebel AI orders only 3 starbase upgrades and no units, the Underworld 2 squads (first tick 4960) plus starbases; most plans are vetoed on Budget (engine/ai). (3) Fleet moves from tick 107: original Rebel 71 hops (first Naboo -> Tatooine, tick 107), Underworld 37 (first Nal Hutta -> Shola, tick 110); port Rebel 0, Underworld 2 (engine/ai). The hands-off Empire fleets also hop twice in the original (Tatooine -> Corellia tick 1334, Kuat -> Corellia 1427); the port's idle human never moves (cause unknown: retreat or a human-side automatic; engine/galactic / apps/sim). (4) Owners: the original Rebel takes Shola from the Underworld at tick 9898; the port has no owner change (engine/ai, follows from 2-3). n/a until the port emits them (engine/apps headless: a per-tick state trace in Q2TRACE format, read by `--port-trace`): initial owners, object counts by type, per-cycle credits, perception. Starbase upgrades are not visible in the trace (no starbase objects in the original's galactic store). No ctest added: the matching metric (cycle timing) is already locked by test_economy. Ready for the Lua-probe traces (`lua_*.txt` in the run dir are read automatically: credits, owners, perception per cycle); skirmish needs `--port-crc` from a port skirmish run. **Lua-probe traces compared (2026-10-30, q2-compare; reader 2, C lines + production.q2q, no Lua output came back):** *Galactic* (20261030-061228, HotM Empire, Medium, seed 0, 13,501 mode ticks; report in its `parity/`): fiscal cycles agree again (1351, 2701, ... 13501); same divergences as the vanilla run: RNG from tick 1 (130 vs 59), production from tick 62 (queues: the original Rebel queues 50 items, first a mining facility at Mon Calamari on tick 89; Underworld 24, first a palace at Felucia on tick 64; the port orders starbase upgrades first and almost no units; engine/ai), fleet moves from tick 104 (engine/ai), owners from tick 7498 (Rebel takes Shola; engine/ai). **New, credits (engine/galactic economy):** the idle Empire has 13,000 at the first C sample (tick 96) and gets +667.5 per cycle from tick 1351, the same income as the port, but the port already shows 13,668 after tick 1: the port pays the frame-0 fiscal cycle, the original does not (its frame-0 cycle runs before the galaxy exists; the trace has no objects at mode frame 0), so the port is exactly one payment ahead all run (final 20,342 vs 19,675). Port credits over time come from short repeated port runs (`--port-exe` samples the trace's C ticks). Queues also show the original's stalled items: an Underworld item whose start frame resets every tick for 400-900 ticks (MDU company at Shola 1538-2827, vehicle factory at Nal Hutta, saboteur team): the ground-cap stall of GE §7 item 6, now observable (engine/galactic production). *Skirmish* (20261030-062751, Geonosis space, Empire idle vs Medium Rebel, seed 0x41ccdfe4): the port has no matching skirmish host. `alamo_headless --skirmish-ai foc --map _MP_SPACE_GEONOSIS --seed 1103945700` is a 3-planet galaxy with a fleet hop into a battle (tactical from CRC record 33), AI on both sides, fixed fleets, no build pads, no tactical credits. Only the RNG is comparable, and only as a rate: per tick 23,275 draws in the original vs 62,466 in the port (2.7x; tick 1 50 vs 155). Trace-only facts: credits start at 6,000 for both sides with no cap; the idle Empire earns about 0.1665 per tick (+299.7 per 1800 ticks); the Rebel AI builds 94 items (mineral extractors from tick 637, a level-2 starbase upgrade at 2470). Open for engine/apps: a real skirmish host (MP map start units and build pads, idle human, start credits, income) and a per-tick state trace (owners, object counts, credits, cycles) to compare these directly |

## Performance, worker-count check and sanitizer limits

Moved on 2026-11-06: the OPT1 / OPT2 / OPT3 measurement sections are in [`PERFORMANCE.md`](PERFORMANCE.md); "Worker-count check" and "Sanitizer limits" are in [`BUILD.md`](BUILD.md).

## BM - repeatable benchmark (BM1, 2026-11-06)

One shared measuring tool for every performance goal: the same game time on every run, and per-tick CRCs that prove each run
did the same work. AI actions are not in the order stream, so the AI must rerun identically from the save; the CRC proves it did.

**Scenario file** (`engine/apps/sim/include/alamo/sim/bench_scenario.hpp`, in `alamo_sim` so `alamo_game` can load the same
scenarios in BM2). A text manifest `<name>.abench` (LF, `key value` lines) next to three files it names:

| Key | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `ALAMO_BENCH 1` | format line; a reader refuses any other format |
| `name`, `version`, `description` | `version` is the scenario version: `--bench-make` keeps it when the remade files are identical and bumps it otherwise; results of different versions are never compared |
| `make <args>` | the `alamo_headless` arguments that made it (`--bench-regen` re-runs them) |
| `save <file> <bytes> <digest>` | the N1 save the run starts from (FNV-1a 64 of the bytes) |
| `orders <file> <bytes> <digest>` | the recorded order stream continuing the save (`from_save`), or `-` |
| `fingerprint <file> <bytes> <digest>` | the expected CRC log from the save tick on (`ALAMO_CRC` 1, or 2 when `fingerprint_every` > 1) |
| `fingerprint_every <N>` | 1 = every tick stored; N = a checkpoint log (every Nth tick, first and last) |
| `start_tick`, `ticks`, `workers` | the save's tick, ticks per run, default workers (`auto` = machine default) |
| `final_crc` | combined CRC after the last tick |

Every pinned file is checked against its size and digest before any timing, so a file changed without remaking the scenario
is caught. The make is deterministic: remaking with nothing changed gives byte-identical files (the test checks it). The save
leaves out the machine's game-data folder (the descriptor's informational `data_root`), so a remake on another machine keeps the
version. All files are written as temporaries and moved in place with the manifest last.

**Commands** (`engine/apps/headless/bench_run.hpp`):
- `alamo_headless --bench S.abench [--runs 3] [--warmup 1] [--workers N] [--ticks K] [--bench-json F|-] [--bench-baseline OLD.json]`:
  sim-only mode. Each run loads the save into a fresh session, replays the orders and runs the ticks as fast as possible (no
  rendering, no pacing). Each tick is timed around `SimHost::tick_once` + the harness step; OPT1's zones are recorded during the timed
  runs. Prints median / p99 (nearest rank) / worst tick per run and over all timed ticks, and the top zones. Every run's CRCs are
  compared with the fingerprint, with run 0, and with `--golden` if given. `tools\benchmark\run_bench.cmd [name]` runs it and writes
  `logs\bench_<name>.json`.
- `--bench-make S.abench --save-session KIND [...] --bench-save-at T --ticks K [--bench-every N] [--bench-desc TEXT]`: builds the
  session (the `--save-session` kinds), saves at T, loads and runs K ticks recording the orders, loads again replaying them and requires
  the same CRCs, which become the fingerprint.
- `--bench-regen S.abench` (`tools\benchmark\regen_scenario.cmd [name]`): remakes it with the recorded `make` arguments.

**Not comparable.** A fingerprint mismatch, a pinned file that differs, or a save that no longer loads prints
`scenario changed, not comparable: <first diverging tick and system>` and exits 3 (0 with `--bench-allow-changed`); the timings are
still printed but labelled, the JSON says `"comparable": false`, and `--bench-baseline` reports no speedup or slowdown for it. Two
results compare only when both matched the same scenario name, version, fingerprint digest and tick count
(`alamo::sim::bench::compare`); a different CPU, compiler, worker count or a noisy side is printed as a note.

**Busy machine.** Each run samples the machine's and the process's CPU time (`GetSystemTimes` / `GetProcessTimes`): when other
processes take more than 10 % of the CPU during a timed run the run and the result are flagged `noisy`. The first space-battle try ran
while other lanes built and tested (CPU at 100 %): the same 1800 ticks took 4.7 s in one run and 161 s in another. Benchmark on an idle
machine.

**JSON result** (`format: alamo-bench-result`, version 1): `scenario` (name, version, session, start tick, ticks, save and fingerprint
digests), `fingerprint` (ok, message), `comparable`, `settings` (ticks, workers requested / used, warm-up and timed runs, rendering off,
pacing, profiler zones), `machine` (CPU, logical cores, memory, OS build, GPU, GPU memory, GPU driver, `gpu_used: false` in sim-only
mode), `build` (compiler, config, git commit at configure time, asserts), `summary` (median / p99 / worst + its tick and run, mean,
ticks per second, `max_other_cpu_pct`, `noisy`), `runs` (the same per run plus load time, wall time, fingerprint, other CPU, final CRC),
`zones` (top 24 OPT1 zones: share, mean, p50, p99, max) and `baseline` (comparable, ratios or the reason, notes). Shared code for
BM2: `bench_stats.hpp` (median / p99 / worst, `compare`) and `bench_json.hpp` (the JSON value, write and read).

**First scenario** `tests/benchmark/space_battle.abench`: `--save-session skirmish --campaign foc-fleet` = the `--skirmish-ai foc` space battle
on `_MP_SPACE_KUAT` with both fleets four times over (20 ships a side: Empire 4 x {Star Destroyer, 2 Tartan, Acclamator, Broadside},
Rebel 4 x {Mon Calamari, Nebulon-B, 2 Corellian Corvette, Assault Frigate}; ships over the unit cap arrive as reinforcements), saved at tick 300 with
the battle running, then 1800 ticks (1 min) of AI-vs-AI battle, which does not end within them. No recorded orders (AI only).
Fingerprint every 16th tick (29 KB; every tick would be 453 KB in the repository): a divergence stays in the state, so it is caught
at the next stored tick, and `first_divergence` names the 16-tick window. Needs `ALAMO_FOC_DATA` (skipped without it).

**Tests.** `alamo_sim_bench_tests` (manifest round trip and errors, pins, statistics, the comparison rule, JSON);
`alamo_headless_bench_synthetic` (`headless/check_bench.cmake`, ~1-3 s, no game data: make a synthetic scenario, bench it with JSON,
remake it unchanged (make line with a quoted `=` argument), compare with a baseline, run 100 of 150 ticks against a
checkpoint fingerprint, reject `--ticks 0`, and refuse a scenario with another run's fingerprint and an edited sidecar as "not
comparable"); `alamo_headless_bench_space_battle*` (slow, FoC data): the OPT3 t07 worker-count check on the space battle from its save
(0 / 1 / 4 / 16 / machine default; `--bench-allow-changed`, so a sim behaviour change only prints "scenario changed" there - remake the
scenario with `tools\benchmark\regen_scenario.cmd` and commit it with the change).

### BM2 - rendered benchmark runs (2026-11-06, group bm2)

**Scenario format 2** (`bench_scenario.hpp`; BM1's format-1 files read unchanged, and a manifest with no format-2 key is still
written as format 1 byte for byte, so `space_battle.abench` and `--bench-regen` are unaffected). After `final_crc`:
`host headless|game` (whose session the save is), `mode realtime|fixed` (default fixed), `preset NAME`, `frames_per_tick K`,
`camera_anchor world|start`, and any number of `camera <tick> <tx> <ty> <tz> <yaw> <pitch> <distance> [<fov>]` keys (ticks
strictly increasing; degrees; yaw about +Z, 0 = camera on +X, not wrapped; pitch -89..89; fov 0 or left out = the view's own).
Floats are written shortest round-trip (`std::to_chars`) and read with `std::from_chars`. A remake keeps the format-2 keys; a
changed camera or setting bumps the version (`same_content` includes them), so results of two camera paths are never compared.
A *recipe* is a manifest with only name / description / format-2 keys (`Scenario::parse_recipe`).

**Camera path** (t02): `camera_pose(keys, camera_time(ticks run, alpha))`, where `camera_time` = 0 before the first measured tick,
else ticks run since the measurement started - 1 + the frame's render alpha (fixed frames: (sub + 1) / K; real time: the live
interpolation alpha). Linear between keys in double (no FMA), held outside them; the fov is linear only when both keys give one.
Never wall-clock time. In alamo_game the pose replaces the drawn view's camera (after the cinematic camera) from the first
gameplay frame on, warm-up included, so the settle check covers the path's first view; it drives the view (galactic or battle)
shown at that first frame, the other view keeps its own camera. `camera_anchor start` adds that view's own camera target at that
frame to every key's target (in fixed frames the presentation clock is frozen during the warm-up, so the anchor is the same
every run).

**alamo_game** (t01, t07; `benchmark_scenario.hpp`): `--bench-scenario S.abench` reads the manifest before the other flags
(which then win: `--benchmark`, `--bench-preset`, `--bench-ticks` up to the scenario's, `--bench-frames-per-tick`,
`--width` / `--height`), checks every pin, loads the save with the recorded orders as the session's replay (`load_game(...,
&replay)`; the run's own commands such as the autopilot's battle choice are ignored by the pipeline: the stream has them at
the same ticks; `crc_check` replays the same stream) and at the end compares the CRC log with the fingerprint (both cut at the
last fingerprint record at or before the end tick): a mismatch is `scenario changed, not comparable` (exit 3, as alamo_headless).
BM2a's named presets stay the default and the override. `--bench-make S.abench --bench-save FILE`: a normal benchmark run; when
it passes with the presentation check matching, it writes S as a `host game` scenario next to `<name>.alsv` (the save's bytes),
`<name>.acmd` (the run's recorded stream, continuing the save) and `<name>.crc` (the run's CRC log, checkpointed every 16 ticks)
with the run's mode / preset / frames per tick / ticks and the existing file's name, description, camera and version (kept when
nothing changed, else +1). The measurement must start at the save's tick (the sim holds during the warm-up).
**Finding: alamo_game cannot run BM1's `space_battle.abench`.** Its save is an alamo_headless `--save-session skirmish` harness
session (descriptor kind `skirmish`, headless participants), not an alamo_game `game.campaign` / `game.skirmish` session, so
`GameSession::spec_from_save` refuses it and the battle could not be drawn through `CampaignBattles` / the battle view anyway. Both
programs now refuse the other's scenarios with the reason before anything loads (`host` key; alamo_headless `--bench` /
`--bench-regen` exit 2 on `host game`). A large game-made space battle scenario is follow-up work.

**GPU timers** (t06): in a benchmark run alamo_game records an `rhi::GpuTimer` per frame: `frame` (the whole command buffer:
world, HUD, gamma, present blit) and the renderer's own scopes (`world`, `scene`, `ao`, `post`, `taa`). A slot's results are
read when FrameRing hands the slot out again (that frame has finished) and kept only when that frame counted; the frames still
in flight are read after a `wait_idle` at the end. Report keys (after `screen`): `scenario`, `scenario_version`, `fingerprint`,
`scenario_made`, `camera_keys`, `gpu_timestamps` (false when the queue has none), `gpu_frames`, `gpu_ms` (per scope: count /
mean / median / p99 / worst / total, GPU ms).

**Tests**: `alamo_sim_bench_tests` (format 2 round trip, format 1 unchanged, bad keys, recipes, camera interpolation),
`alamo_game_benchmark_tests` (flags and settings from a scenario, overrides, make / remake versions, fingerprint check, the
headless refusal, report keys), `alamo_game_bench_scenario_headless` (no data: alamo_game refuses `space_battle.abench`) and
`alamo_game_bench_scenario` (slow, FoC data; `bench_scenario_check.cmake`): `--bench-make` on a copy of
`engine/apps/game/testdata/bench_orbit.abench` from the saveload smoke's battle save, then two `--bench-scenario` runs: fingerprint
match, 120 counted frames, GPU scopes, identical PNGs in both runs, the camera moved (first and last PNG differ), and a changed
pinned file refused.

### BM3 - stress scenes, load scenes and reports (2026-11-06, group bm3)

**The scenes** (`tests/benchmark/`; every one is a committed scenario: `.abench` + `.alsv` + `.acmd` + `.crc`, none carries a
machine-specific path). Reference timings are in [`PERFORMANCE.md`](PERFORMANCE.md) ("Benchmark scenes").

| Scene | Host | What it stresses | Made with | Length |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `space_battle` (BM1) | headless | AI-vs-AI space battle, 4x fleets | `--skirmish-ai foc-fleet` | 1800 ticks |
| `galactic_late` (t01) | headless | late-game galaxy, all AI: FoC `Sandbox_Equal_Footing_Underworld` (56 planets, 4 AI players), saved at tick 30000: galactic AI goal scoring, auto-resolved conflicts, story Lua | `--save-session galactic --campaign ... --battles auto --story` | 27000 ticks (15 game minutes) |
| `land_battle` (t02) | headless | one large land battle (pathfinding, infantry, vehicles, tactical AI): `_MP_LAND_UTAPAU`, Empire vs Rebel, six times the `foc-land` company lists | `--skirmish-ai foc-land-army` (new kind, `kArmyScale` = 6) | 3600 ticks (2 game minutes) |
| `space_battle_game` (bm2c t01) | game | rendered large space battle: `_MP_SPACE_KUAT`, an idle Empire fleet against the Rebel AI, x6, the camera circles the fight | `--bench-new-save` | 1800 ticks, 3600 frames (fixed, 2 per tick, `bm-high`) |
| `nebula_battle` (t03) | game | explosions + nebula / asteroid overdraw: `_MP_SPACE_DAGOBAH`, x6, the camera flies into the nebula in front of the fight | `--bench-new-save` | same |
| `water_battle` (t03) | game | water reflection / refraction + rain: `_MP_LAND_KAMINO` (60 % of the grid under water, reflection and refraction 1.0), x4 ground forces, low camera | `--bench-new-save` | same |

The headless scenes run with `alamo_headless --bench tests/benchmark/<name>.abench` (BM1; `tools\benchmark\run_bench.cmd <name>`), the
rendered ones with `alamo_game --hidden --bench-scenario tests/benchmark/<name>.abench` (BM2; add `--bench-report FILE`).

**Why the rendered scenes are game-made.** `alamo_game` cannot load an `alamo_headless` harness save, and a game save of the attract
demo cannot be made, so the rendered scenes start from a *skirmish session built by alamo_game's own `GameSession`*:
`alamo_game --bench-new-save FILE --bench-map MAP [--bench-human F] [--bench-ai F] [--bench-scale N] [--bench-save-after T]
[--bench-seed S]` (no window, no GPU: `benchmark_save.hpp`) starts the skirmish (the human side's default fleet or army repeated
N times against the AI's, N 1..16), runs it until the battle is on the mode stack plus T ticks and writes the save. The human side takes
no orders in a benchmark (it only defends; the AI side attacks and keeps reinforcing). The descriptor's game-data folder is cleared and
the save has no wall-clock time, so the same arguments make the same bytes on any machine. `alamo_game --bench-maps` lists the
official skirmish maps with what a scene needs to pick one (space / land, grid, water type / height / share of vertices under it,
reflection and refraction settings, sky domes, weather, and the positions of placed nebula / cloud / field objects); `--bench-trace N`
(a benchmark run) prints every Nth tick each owner's object count, centroid and bounding box, which is how the camera paths were
aimed.

**A game scenario's recipe is its manifest**: the committed `.abench` carries a `make` line (the `--bench-new-save` arguments minus the
output) plus the format-2 keys (`host game`, `mode`, `preset`, `frames_per_tick`, `camera_anchor`, `camera ...`).

**Regenerate** (after a save-format, sim-behaviour or game-data change: "scenario changed, not comparable", exit 3):
`alamo_game --hidden --bench-regen tests/benchmark/<name>.abench` remakes the session from the `make` line, runs
`--bench-make` on it and bumps `version` when the files changed (headless scenes: `tools\benchmark\regen_scenario.cmd <name>`).
`tools\regen_goldens.cmd --bench-check` checks **every** scene with `alamo_headless --bench --runs 1 --warmup 0` (game ones sim-only
since bm2d t02; a game scene whose save no longer loads exits 3, not comparable, bm2d t01) and `tools\regen_goldens.cmd --only bench`
remakes the stale ones (game ones with `alamo_game --hidden --bench-regen`); never by hand.
New scene: write the recipe (a manifest with `name`, `description`, the `make` line and the format-2 keys), run
`alamo_game --hidden --bench-regen` on it (or `alamo_headless --bench-make`), commit the four files.

**Rebaseline** (a result file is a baseline for one scenario version on one PC): after an intended speed change run
`tools\benchmark\run_suite.cmd` on a quiet machine (nothing else building or testing; a run is flagged NOISY when the CPU was busy
before it), keep the JSON files under `logs\bench_suite\`, and compare against the previous ones with
`python tools\benchmark\compare_results.py BEFORE.json AFTER.json`. Record the new numbers in `PERFORMANCE.md` with the commit.
Never record a noisy run as a baseline.

**Result comparison** (t06; `tools/benchmark/compare_results.py`, self-test `test_compare_results.py`, ctest `alamo_bench_compare_tool`):
reads an `alamo-bench-result` (headless) or `alamo_game_benchmark` (game) file pair and prints metric / before / after / change / verdict
(`faster`, `SLOWER`, `same` within `--threshold` percent, default 3; headless also the biggest profiler zones; game also the GPU scopes,
the load time and the hitch count). It **refuses** (exit 2) two files of different kinds, scenario name, version, save or fingerprint
digest, a run that was not comparable with its scenario, and (game) a different mode / preset / size / frames per tick / tick count, each
with its own override (`--allow-different-scenario`, `--allow-different-settings`); a different machine or build is only a note
(`--allow-different-machine` silences it). A noisy run is marked and never counts for `--fail-slower PCT` (exit 4).

**Load scenes and first-use hitches** (t04): a rendered run now reports `load_ms` (from the save's load start to the first measured
tick: the load screen, warm-up frames and every pipeline / model / texture the first view waits for), `early_ms` (statistics of the
first 300 counted frames) and `hitches` (counted frames over max(25 ms, 4 x the median): their count and the worst eight as
`[frame index, ms]`). `tools\benchmark\run_suite.cmd --load` runs the rendered scenes in real time (`--benchmark realtime
--bench-ticks 300`) with a cold cache (a fresh empty `--cache-dir`: pipelines and shaders compile during the run) and then a warm one
(the same directory again); the pairs show the cold-cache hitches. In fixed-frames runs the clock waits for the renderer to settle, so
hitches only show in real-time mode.

**Noise** (t05; `tools\benchmark\run_suite.cmd [--repeats N] [--scenes a,b]`): each scene N times as separate processes; per headline
metric the min / max / mean, spread ((max - min) / mean) and coefficient of variation go to `logs\bench_suite\suite.json` and the
console. See `PERFORMANCE.md` for the figures on the reference PC.

**Tests**: `alamo_game_benchmark_tests` (extended: report keys `load_ms` / `early_ms` / `hitches`, hitch detection, the
`--bench-new-save` / `--bench-maps` flags), `alamo_bench_compare_tool` (compare tool on synthetic files, no data),
`alamo_headless_bench_galactic_late*` and `alamo_headless_bench_land_battle*` (slow, FoC data: CRCs of 1 / 4 / N workers equal the 0-worker
run), `alamo_game_bench_scene_<name>` (slow, FoC data, `bench_scene_check.cmake`: 120 hidden ticks per rendered scene, fingerprint match,
report keys).

### BM2c - sim-only timing of game scenes, TAA scenario run (2026-11-08, group bm2c)

**Sim-only timing of a `host game` scene** (t02). Port choice: `alamo_headless --bench` runs it, linked with the render-free
`alamo_game_core` (`engine/apps/headless/bench_game.cpp`, compiled with `ALAMO_HEADLESS_HAVE_GAME_CORE` when that target exists;
wired in `engine/apps/CMakeLists.txt` after `add_subdirectory(game)`), instead of a new sim-only mode in `alamo_game`: the BM1 runner
(warm-up / timed runs, worker count, fingerprint and run-to-run checks, OPT1 zones, `--crc-out` / `--golden`, `--bench-baseline`, the
`alamo-bench-result` JSON) applies unchanged, so a game scene's result has exactly BM1's shape (`tool alamo_headless`, `mode sim-only`,
`scenario.session` e.g. `game.skirmish '...' seed 1 map _MP_SPACE_KUAT`) and `tools/benchmark/compare_results.py` compares it like a
headless one. `open_game_save_source` reads the save once and loads the game data once (`--game`, else `ALAMO_FOC_DATA`; skipped
without; the save's VFS arguments unless given); each run builds a fresh `GameSession` with its own job system from the save
(`GameSession::spec_from_save` + `start_from_save`, the path of `load_game` and of alamo_game's presentation on/off check) with the
scenario's orders as the replay, and each timed tick is one `GameSession::tick`. The fingerprint is the CRC log of the rendered run that
made the scene, so a matching sim-only run did the rendered run's sim work (the check also compares its final CRC with the manifest's
`final_crc`). An `alamo_headless` built without `alamo_game_core` still refuses a game scene with the reason.
`alamo_headless --bench tests/benchmark/space_battle_game.abench [--runs R] [--bench-json F]`.

**TAA scenario run** (t03). `engine/apps/game/testdata/bench_orbit_taa.abench` is `bench_orbit.abench` on `bm-high` (TAA Medium);
`bench_scenario_check.cmake -DTAA=1` makes it from the saveload smoke's battle save and runs it twice at 640x360, run b with
`--bench-redraw-every 3` (BM2b): both reports say `taa: true`, run b reports the forced redraws and as many TAA discards, and the PNGs
of the two runs are identical.

**Tests**: `alamo_headless_bench_game_<scene>` for `space_battle_game`, `nebula_battle`, `water_battle` (slow, FoC data,
`headless/check_bench_game.cmake`: one full-length timed run, exit 0, BM1 JSON keys, every tick timed, final CRC = the manifest's);
`alamo_headless_bench_space_battle_game*` (slow, FoC data: the worker-count check, 0 / 1 / 4 / 16 / machine default);
`alamo_game_bench_scenario_taa` (slow, FoC data).

## Q4 parity baseline (q4h t02, 2026-11-07; re-recorded after a2k t10 / t11, q4hf t01)

Measured with the Q4 harness (`tools/parity/`, README there: metrics, tolerances and their reasons, grading). The table is
the current `tools/parity/baseline.json`, recorded at **`d3237074`** (q4hf t03, below); before that **`dc141745`** (q4hf t02 / t04 merged with dev's q2f t08, which grades
`hotm_galactic` against 2 retail runs, and cu8j t01-t03; alamo_headless MSVC Release, 4 workers; 0 regressions against
dev's `acb3547a` and cu8j's `a956c954` recordings; `hotm_galactic` is the same as at `207ba0b8`); before that `4b6aa69d` (q4hf t02 / t04, 3 retail runs), dev `79576620` (a2k t10 + t11)
and dev `3629d4cf`, before the a2k galactic-AI merge (row "q4h t02" below; the harness has more timing metrics since). Re-record it, like a golden, when an intended behaviour change moves a metric. The confirm, AI
and Q4 groups are judged against these numbers. Reference runs: the Q2 traces in the main checkout's
`tools/probe_mod/q2_traces/out/`.

**2026-11-08 (q2f t08): `hotm_galactic` now uses 2 retail runs.** `20261103-152619-galactic-vanilla-lua` was dropped:
its Lua plan hooks killed retail's own plan threads (`docs/re/LUA_SCRIPT_OUTPUT.md` §4), so its AI production and fleet
moves were not retail behaviour, and it widened the AI envelopes (Rebel fleet departures 19..71). `baseline.json` was
re-recorded at `acb3547a` (same engine code as dev `03940b96`). Graded against the old 3 runs the port had 0
regressions; all 13 new FAILs come from the narrower envelope, among them `ai.fleet_departures[Rebel]` 30 vs 71,
`ai.fleet_targets[Rebel]` overlap 0.23 (retail shuttles Shola / Naboo), `ai.build_mix[Rebel]` 0.44 (retail builds 22
infantry squads, the port 2). `ai.build_count[Rebel]` is 40 vs retail 50 (PASS, tol 12.5).

**2026-11-08 (q2f t10): `hotm_galactic` uses 3 retail runs again.** The fixed Lua probe's rerun
`20261108-170824-galactic-vanilla-lua` is clean (no record gaps, retail AI undisturbed) and was added. `baseline.json`
re-recorded at `b4da58e1` (engine = dev `432b502e`, after a2l): 0 regressions against dev's baseline (`506bc545`),
2 improved (`econ.credits_at_13500[Underworld]`, `econ.income_mean[Underworld]`). Note: `ai.build_count[Rebel]` is 19
vs retail 50..51 at this engine (it was 40 at `03940b96`; already FAIL in the `506bc545` baseline), Rebel fleet
departures 16 vs 55..63. Findings for q2f t09: `docs/re/Q2F_T09_REBEL_PRODUCTION.md`, "2026-11-08 rerun".

**2026-11-08 (q2f t09): synchronized free-store gather** (`docs/re/Q2F_T09_RE_ANSWERS.md` Q2; marked "q2f t09" in
`engine/ai/src/galactic_ai.cpp`). `Produce_Force(stage, true)` (the shipped `SynchronizedAssemble`: ContrastToReconnect,
LiftBlockadePlan) now holds every unit until the task force's builds are done and no unit is in transit, gives each unit
not at the stage an ETA = all-pairs path length (0x691370) / (hyperspace speed x faction factor x fleet speed multiplier
x FPS), starts the countdown at the largest ETA and departs a unit (split from a shared fleet first) when the countdown
is at or below its ETA. [port] choices: the mode speed scale m is read as the fleet speed multiplier (as in the
build-market travel term); a company without its own hyperspace speed uses its transport's; a unit whose ETA fails is
released from the task force (retail: that build task's Production_Failed); the countdown drops by the frames elapsed
since the last pass / 30. AI world save version 7 (6 still read; the countdown is hashed only for sync task forces).
Golden: `galactic_ai_hotm_human_golden.txt` (a Lift_Blockade gather at Kashyyyk now departs staggered); HotM seed 0
(q4h parity) is unchanged, since no Rebel plan there gathers with sync. Not ported: mode 0's refusal to end on an
enemy-held planet (Q1.5), the arrival barrier before the merge (Q2.5).
2026-11-08 follow-up (q2f t09): a sync departure from a shared fleet used to mark the fleet busy after one split, so one
unit per fleet left per AI pass (3 ticks) and a wave landed in up to five groups, each auto-resolved and lost alone. Now
every unit due on a pass splits (PRE_PATH_SEPERATE runs op 2 / op 0 directly, Q2.2) and its own fleet is pathed on the
same pass; [port] when the split has not taken effect yet (AI latency pipeline) the fleet stays busy and the unit paths on
the next pass. HotM Empire-human seeds 0-11: Shola taken by tick 13501 in 7 of 12 (before: the 2 seeds whose first
Rebel space build is a Marauder, via the early Conquer_Pirate); seeds 3 / 4 at 7626 / 7647 (retail 7498). Golden
`galactic_ai_hotm_human_golden.txt` regenerated (the Kuat split and move on one tick); q4h parity 0 regressions.

**2026-11-08 (q4hf t03): more retail references.** `geonosis_skirmish` now has 3 retail runs (`20261108-204213-skirmish-vanilla-lua`,
`20261108-203639-skirmish-vanilla` added), so its tolerances use the run-to-run envelope; `hotm_galactic` has a 4th run,
`20261108-204711-galactic-vanilla-lua` (40 cycles, cut at the scenario's `max_tick` 13501; its two auto-resolved Tatooine

q4hf t04 (harness hop segmentation, `tools/parity/README.md` "Metrics"): a stop needs 3 equal samples, so one repeated
retail sample mid-flight no longer ends a hop (retail The Maw -> Kashyyyk 122 was the tail of a 162-tick leg, Yavin ->
The Maw 29 the tail of a 62-tick one); a trip through a planet centre is split into legs (port Nal Hutta -> The Maw 133 =
Nal Hutta -> Kashyyyk + Kashyyyk -> The Maw); a leg runs from the last sample at its start planet to the first at its end
planet on both sides (retail orbit slot / port centre, so no one-tick offset). Travel is now 40 of 41 legs PASS, nearly
all exact. The one FAIL is real: Underworld Nal Hutta -> Kashyyyk 25 vs retail 21 in all 3 runs (the port's fleet flies
that lane at 2.62 per tick, retail at 3.15; engine/galactic hyperspace speed). The earlier lead "the port's Underworld
fleets move at Rebel speed" (bullet below) does not hold: those three off routes were segmentation artifacts and match
now. The other 4 timing FAILs are builds: `REBEL_INFANTRY_SQUAD` at Kamino / Naboo 276 / 240 vs 140 / 116,
`REBEL_TANK_BUSTER_SQUAD@NABOO` 342 vs 173, `UNDERWORLD_DISRUPTOR_MERC_SQUAD@FELUCIA` 878 vs 242 (one retail run each).
Fleet departures and targets now count trips (stop to stop), still 16 / 0.

q4hf t02 (skirmish damage hook): the skirmish host writes its tactical battle as a Q2TRACE 1 trace (`--trace-out`,
`engine/apps/headless/tactical_trace.hpp`), reduced by the retail reader. `combat.damage_taken`: Rebel 308 hull lost vs
retail 325 (PASS, 25%), Empire 0 vs 0 (PASS); `combat.first_shot_frame` 2450 vs 3953 (FAIL: the port's Rebel AI attacks
about 50 s earlier, as its early X-wing build already suggested). `outcome.units_final[Rebel]` 41 vs 35 is now within
tolerance (already PASS on dev `03940b96` before this change, so moved by a behaviour change after `79576620`). After cu8j t01-t03 (formation
composition / adopt draws, `dc141745`): Rebel hull lost 339 vs 325 (PASS), first shot 2150 vs 3953 (FAIL),
`outcome.units_final[Rebel]` 44 vs 35 and `units_lost[Empire]` 8 vs 0 FAIL (the AI fight moves with the draws).

a2k t10 (Add_Credits AI notify, `docs/re/A2K_T10_CREDIT_NOTIFY_RE.md`): `econ.credits_at_9000[Rebel]` 3106 -> 867 (retail
650..1143, now PASS; the Rebel spends the Shola capture bonus within ~170 ticks). Still FAIL: `credits_at_4500[Rebel]` 1186
(retail 677..825), `credits_at_9000[Underworld]` 324 (retail 2462..2849; plan choice / trajectory, saboteur question open,
a2k t05). New FAIL: `credits_at_13500[Rebel]` 1169 (retail 23..899) and `income_mean[Rebel]` 812 (retail 713..736): the
port's Rebel holds both Shola and a MonCalimari mining facility, a combination neither retail run had. Also
`build.ticks[UNDERWORLD_MERC_SQUAD@NALHUTTA]` FAIL -> PASS. The bullets below describe the q4h t02 run.

The leave-one-out row grades each retail run like the port against the other two: the three HotM runs (same seed 0,
no input) already differ from tick 140 on, so a port FAIL in an AI-driven metric has to be read against that row.

Differences outside tolerance (port vs retail):

- **Timing, galactic.** Fiscal cycles: all 10 exact. Builds: 8 of 10 type/planet pairs within 5% (mining facility,
  palace, barracks, factories, turbolaser towers: port 600 / 720 / 1440, retail 602 / 722 / 1442, i.e. retail items
  leave the queue 2 ticks after start + frames); `CORELLIAN_GUNBOAT@NABOO` 432 vs 458-482 and
  `UNDERWORLD_SABOTEUR_TEAM@NALHUTTA` 540 vs 602 are shorter in the port (engine/galactic production build-time
  modifiers). Hops: all 7 Rebel routes within 3 ticks (e.g. Shola -> Nal Hutta 160 vs 161, Kamino -> The Maw 277 vs 279);
  3 Underworld routes off (Nal Hutta -> Shola 160 vs retail 80 / 89 / 107, Felucia -> Kashyyyk 68 vs 104, Kashyyyk -> Kuat
  28 vs 44): the port's Underworld fleets move at Rebel speed where retail is faster on some hops and slower on others
  (engine/galactic hyperspace speed: corruption bonus, fleet composition).
- **Income, galactic.** The idle Empire matches exactly (667.5 per cycle, 15,002.5 / 17,005 / 19,007.5 at ticks 4500 /
  9000 / 13500). Underworld first payment 2105 vs 2150-2240, Underworld ends at 0 credits (retail 176-312), Rebel mean
  payment 817 vs 713-736 (engine/galactic income + engine/ai spending).
- **AI choices, galactic.** Rebel queues 11 items (retail 32-50), type overlap 0.22 (retail runs among themselves 0.30):
  the q2f t09 gap (no infantry, tank busters, Y-wings). Underworld production is within tolerance (23 items, overlap 0.54),
  but its fleets leave first at tick 1963 (retail 110) and make 13 departures (retail 37-56), target overlap 0.19 (engine/ai
  galactic plans). First builds within 4 ticks of retail (Rebel 87 vs 89, Underworld 68 vs 64).
- **Battle outcomes, galactic.** Final owners within the retail outcomes (4/4/4); 2 owner changes (retail 0-1).
- **Skirmish (Geonosis, idle Empire vs Rebel AI).** The idle human's credits match to 0.02 (6299.7 / 6599.4 / 6899.1). The
  port's Empire gains 7 TIE fighters and 4 TIE bombers it never builds (retail: none; engine/apps skirmish host or starbase
  spawns) and loses 3 units (retail 0: the Rebel AI attacks earlier). The Rebel AI builds X-wings early (frame 600, retail
  1800), 38 items (retail 25), no mineral extractors (retail 5), balance 2750 / 100 / 400 vs 226 / 1256 / 2112 at frames
  1800 / 3600 / 5400 (engine/ai tactical production).

Gaps: the skirmish damage hook exists since q4hf t02 (above); no retail
battle-outcome trace (hands-off galactic runs stop when a tactical battle starts); one retail skirmish run only (no spread);
galactic object counts by type are not comparable (members vs companies).

## Port choices and per-step notes (tracked on the roadmap)

This list holds the [port] / [inferred] / [M] choices and per-step notes. Finished ("✅ DONE") items were moved verbatim to `docs/PHASE4_HISTORY.md` on 2026-11-06 (section "Finished open-item notes moved from PHASE4.md"). Which items are still open is decided on the main roadmap, not by the tags here (the tag legend and counts that were here were moved verbatim to `docs/PHASE4_HISTORY.md` on 2026-11-06). Inline, ✅ marks a finished sub-item, often also struck through, and ⬜ marks one still open.

- ⬜ **OPEN** · cu8f t01 / t02 / t07 (2026-11-07, spec `docs/re/CU8F_T01_T02_T07_RE.md`): [port] choices (marked "cu8f" in code) — **t01** SAP +0x28: `Cancel_Special_Ability` accepts any non-dead ability object whose owner is not allied to the user's ("has a SAP" = known to AbilitySystem); its register is the special's `victims` list (remove enables each); the disable keeps the cancelled effect's target in `SpecialState::restore` (an aura's = its owner) and the enable re-applies only those cancelled effects (non-automatic effects left applied while disabled are not re-applied); the dropped due-frame entry of a cancelled special is left as it was (the enable re-stagger overwrites it for every enabled interval special); with no bound RNG the re-stagger due = now; the flag stays in the ability save (the original does not save it, so a loaded original game starts enabled) because the port also saves the Cancel effect whose remove clears it — save version 3 → 4 (restore target); a host `remove()` of the user enables at frame 0. **t02** energy-pool engine disable: `CombatHooks::engines_drained` → `TacticalBattle::energy_drain_engines` inside Take_Damage; the move stop ignores the last waypoint's flag 0x10 [M] and signal 0x16 is not modelled. HOLSTER_WEAPON / Saber_Throw engine-loop suppression: ported in cu8g t01 (entry below). **t07** Force-disable emitter: emitters need DISABLE_FORCE_ABILITIES in the object's current behaviour list, victims SELECTABLE, distance² < r² (strict); the space retreat fallback counts live, not-retreated battle units of the requesting player (combat owner, else the store's) whose type has TACTICAL_SUPER_WEAPON in its space list.
- ⬜ **OPEN** · cu8i t01 / t02 (2026-11-08, specs `docs/re/CU8F_T01_T02_T07_RE.md` §2.6, `docs/re/CU8G_T02_ENV_PICK_RE.md` §7 / §8 item 7, `docs/re/MOVEMENT.md` §1.6 / §4.1): [port] choices (marked "cu8i" in code), superseding the cu8g t01 "thrown saber is never in flight" / "catch not called" lines below. **t01 Saber_Throw flight:** the projectile is not spawned (no sim object, nothing in the CRC); a stand-in flight of `saber_flight_frames(d, v, Catch_Dist)` = ceil(d / v) + ceil(max(d − Catch_Dist, 0) / v) frames (at least 1), d = the XY distance owner → target at the ability apply, v = the `Saber_Name` type's Max_Speed per frame (Lukes_Light_Saber 8, Catch_Dist 15: 150 units → 36 frames, retail 35; 120 units → 29, retail 43 with a moving target), carried as the `Unhandled` SABER_THROW event's value (value 0 = the forwarding path's failed activation: no throw). The throw still runs at the apply (retail: at anim 0x6c's half). `EngineLoops` keeps the catch frame (save version 2 → 3; version 2 is still read, with no saber in flight); `hg_holster_service` passes `saber_in_flight` up to and including the catch frame, then calls `saber_catch` on it, so the held draw transition clears and the hum is unsuppressed the frame after the catch (retail `luke` runs). Audio only: no golden moved for t01. **t02 FormationClass ctor draw:** every land formation the move split creates draws one synced `int(1, 30)` (the ctor's next-check timer +0x1d8 := now + FPS + r) in formation order, replacing the [inferred] `Get_Int(0, Max_Move_Frame_Delay)`; the §1.6 [M] start formula is read with that offset: start = now + accumulated PathFrameDelay + (FPS + r) − 2·FPS, not before now (so with no PathFrameDelay, vanilla almost always, formations start at once). Queued orders (flag 0x08) add a block to the existing formations [M]: no ctor draw, the per-unit `[0, Max_Move_Frame_Delay]` delays stay. Load_Map tail (land, after the environment pick, before every post-load step): one ctor draw per object with the LOCOMOTOR behaviour (TacticalMode::movable) that has no movement coordinator (`MovementSystem::has_coordinator`: an order or a formation), in store insertion order, whatever the owner: the port's inert Is_Neutral map units count (`_MP_LAND_ENDOR`: its one Land_Speeder = the retail trace's 1 draw; `_MP_LAND_TATOOINE` 14, `_MP_LAND_DAGOBAH` 0). The new formations hold no order and are not kept. Not ported (follow-up): space formation ctor draws (move_space split, fighter formations, the 0x2a1eb0 post-load creations), formation creations outside a move order (unit creation / reinforcement paths, the jetpack swap re-issue, the special-locomotor hold).
- ⬜ **OPEN** · cu8j t01 / t02 / t03 (2026-11-08, specs `docs/re/MOVEMENT.md` §1.6 / §4.1 / §4.4 / §4.6 / §4.7, `docs/re/CU8F_T01_T02_T07_RE.md` §2.6 / §2.7, `docs/re/CU8G_T02_ENV_PICK_RE.md` §7 / §8 item 7): [port] choices (marked "cu8j" in code), **correcting the cu8i t02 line above** (its start formula and draw order were wrong). **Correction (t01):** the FormationClass ctor draw r = int(1, FPS) only sets the next-check timer F +0x1d8 = now + 30 + r; the land start offset is a separate r' = int(0, PathFrameDelay) of the seed, drawn only for seeds with PathFrameDelay ≠ 0, in formation order, before all the ctor draws (0x43c1b0, then 0x43e5b0). The plan gate is F +0x1dc = now + acc + r' − 2·FPS, so a staggered formation plans first at now + acc + r' + 1; an unstaggered one (gate 0) at once, except that one created at battle frame ≤ 60 waits until frame 61 [M]. **Composition at the next coordinator update:** `MovementSystem::move` (not queued, not internal) registers a pending composition (serial, land / space, goal, formations with member ids, space start offsets); its members get `start_frame = kStartPending` and draw nothing. `coordinator_update(frame, global_frame, rng)` composes them in creation order: the tactical host calls it at tick step 10 (after the movement tick, before the victory check: `set_deferred_composition(true)`), the standalone system at the start of `tick()`. So a battle order's units move from the tick after the order; a fighter's path reset (enter_idle) keeps an order still waiting for its composition (retail: the formation's Set_Move comes after it); a later order before the update supersedes the members (`pend` serial). Saved (movement save version 12 → 13: per unit `pend` / `adopted`, the stationary layer, the pending list). **t01 space:** `compose_space` = 0x5d58f0: candidates in the order's unit order, a squadron's fighters as one candidate (its container); seed = the candidate nearest the goal (strict <); one draw per formation; a seed with a role (Stationary_Space_Layer when not following a path and set, else Space_Layer) is one formation; a role-0 seed recruits role-0 candidates in candidate order within the box |dx| ≤ max(2·ex_seed, 0.33·|goal − seed|) + ex_c (same for y; extents = Custom_Hard_X/YExtent, else the radius [M]) whose speed ratios Min/Max ≤ 0.9 both ways; a seed without speed stops the pass (kept for the next update). A fighter formation's first 0x4192b0 / 0x410a60 check is creation + 30 + r (`f.next`, kStartPending until then; `fighter_new_order` composes too, without moving anyone). Not modelled: the locked coordinator flag (0x39ad40), multi-locomotor space units. **t02 adopt 0x39bcb0** (`MovementSystem::adopt`, host `mv_adopt`): one draw at once for an object without a coordinator that passes the predicate (no team member; the first *LOCOMOTOR token of the type's behaviours decides the kind: WALK / BIKE / JETPACK / LAND_TEAM_* = land 1, SIMPLE_SPACE / FIGHTER / FLYING / LANDBOMBER = space 2, TEAM_LOCOMOTOR = 2 when a live member has a kind; it must be the mode's kind, so land Team containers never draw); an object outside the movement system draws every time it qualifies [port]. Sites: Load_Map tail (land and space, every object); the team helper per container right after `add_container`, in chunk order (skirmish: only when the first member has Create_Team; campaign / reinforcements: always), so the draws interleave as retail's; the campaign spawn (galactic start forces, not reserves / script spawns): each member not in a team; space campaign start: fleet placement 0x4fbb10, a second adopt (leave + adopt) per registered object of a group whose first member's Ship_Class is 0..6 [inferred: unsigned bound], after the side's deploys (positions not modelled); the hangar squadron spawn (container); the garrison leave; the stop-and-hold 0x3a9880 (`stop_and_hold`: stop, leave, adopt: always one draw) for Force Sight (Force Reveal begin, the one caller the spec names) through the StopOrder event value `kStopHold`; DEPLOY (0x3a1ce0) stops then adopts (`kStopAdopt`). The jetpack swap to the secondary registers a land composition of that unit alone: one draw at the next update (none for the swap back). Script company spawns adopt nothing [M]. Not ported (follow-up): the other 0x3a9880 callers (unidentified), the lock counter, container eject, landing, fighter hyperspace finish, space retreat, REPLENISH_WINGMEN / indigenous spawns, reinforcement landing re-adopts. **t03 Saber_Throw, the fallback** (§2.7 item 5; the projectile was judged more than a day: a new LIGHT_SABER projectile category with per-step 23-unit area damage, a host object spawn, the two-leg spline plan and Luke's animation-driven SWORD_THROW / CONTROL / CATCH machine across combat, abilities, anim and the host): `ta::saber_flight` (the jitter-free stand-in: Ltot = 2·|T − P0| in 3D with T = the target point at the apply and P0 = L = the thrower, B = the Saber_Name type's Projectile_Max_Flight_Distance + the target radius + |ΔZ|; caught at k_c = floor((Ltot − Catch_Dist) / v) + 1 when v·k_c < min(Ltot, B), end = k_c + 11; else end = min(ceil(Ltot / v), ceil(B / v)), ε = 0 [M]); the throw `kSaberThrowDelay` = 7 frames after the apply [M: 7 or 8]. `EngineLoops::saber_schedule` / `saber_service`: snap holstered at the throw, in flight from the throw to the ability's end, snap drawn only on a real catch, the hum back the frame after the end (EngineLoops save version 3 → 4; 2 and 3 still read). Audio only: the saber deals no damage and takes no path draws (follow-up). **Goldens** (regenerated, not hand-edited): the extra composition / adopt draws move the rng and movement columns first and then most systems in the headless skirmish AI goldens (foc, foc land, foc land campaign, synthetic), the game smokes (skirmish, campaign, land: .crc and .rec), the sim battle goldens (bm, queue, synthetic, l1) and the benchmark scenarios space_battle (v9 → v10), land_battle, space_battle_game, nebula_battle and water_battle (v2 → v3; their saves no longer load after movement save 13, so the game ones were remade with `alamo_game --hidden --bench-regen`); the galactic-only sim goldens, galactic_late and the replay golden are unchanged. Parity baseline re-recorded: geonosis_skirmish `outcome.units_lost[Empire]` PASS → FAIL (port 8, retail 0, tol 2; the AI fight moves with the draws), hotm_galactic unchanged. The skirmish and land smokes still reach combat.

[port] choices: both rules also apply on a pushed path (the same speed step; on a crush-flee path x Fleeing_Infantry_Speed_Bonus with the team speed); a collision hold (`hold_until`) stops the container in one frame (§7.7 item 3: the collision cap may demand an immediate change), it does not brake at 0x5ab540; only live steering members count (parked / garrisoned members and members suspended for a hero clash are not in the port's list; retail counts every TEAM list entry); ~~the other terms of item 3 are not modelled: the port brakes with the walk's sqrt(2 x deceleration x remaining) curve and stops on the last waypoint from a few steps of speed (start ramp exact, braking 13 frames instead of 16 for the Stormtrooper squad), a Stop or a new order still zeroes the speed at once, no "T := the deceleration" in state 0x14;~~ (item 3 in full through `walk_speed`, s78n t26.2); A member held by its own speed factor 0 (stun) holds the container like in retail. - ⬜ **OPEN** · s78n t18 (2026-11-10, specs `docs/re/MOVEMENT.md` §5.2 / §5.3 / §5.3.1 / §5.3.2 / §5.4.1 / §5.4.2, `docs/re/FIGHTER_FLYING.md` §7.4 / §7.5 / §7.7 / §7.8): (marked "s78n t18" in code) squad collision, speed and garrison follow-ups; replaces the overruled [port] choices of the t14 part B and t16 lines. **Done to spec:** container teleport moves its members (0x3abb80); one layer predicate (`land_layer_object`) for candidates, crowd, push spot, repath obstacles and the jetpack landing test; the live joint slot box as the squad's footprint (`team_joint_box`) and the three contact tests of §5.3.1; records serviced every frame and ended by their state; the MovingVsStopped decision 0x684f90 (Repulsion, early-arrival cut, Path Around, push, crusher state 4, Try Again Later every 30 frames; no shove for any pair); the Infantry / Bike MovingVsMoving chooser (Path Around for a forced path, Speed Slave Only, Repulse); the steering vector's sides, radius and side tests, also for a Walk unit that is no squad member; `team_walk` (step order of 0x5aaf70, braking at the rate, arrival within 5.0 with no snap, final-facing turn, coast after a new order); TEAM entries (`team_entries`) in the box, the rate and the cap; squads garrison as one (`GarrisonSystem::set_group_fn`: the container type's Garrison_Value, default 1, and Garrison_Category; 3-D range minus the members' union-box radius; 60.0 once the approach move ended); pair gates (`set_pair_gate`, `set_garrison_exit`, hover state) in place of `set_collision_exempt`; MovementPredictionInterval widens the MovingVsStopped look-ahead of every mover type (review; before: squad containers only - the 160 x 160 candidate box still bounds how early a record can appear). "the approximation reaches the path end" (destination blocked) = the path end is within the look-ahead distance + the two footprint radii of the mover in a straight line; ~~state 1 answers max speed (no formation-follow speed)~~ (s78n t20 batch 1); step 10's units-ignoring detour 0x685490 is a stand-in: the mover keeps its path and the obstacle counts as planned around, no attack block; the early arrival's "at once" flag is set for the tick of the cut. ~~*MovingVsMoving:* the same-formation branch (states 11 / 13 / Pass), the starvation states 15 / 16 and the (1, 1) flags of an undecided record are not modelled; a squad container in such a pair is never side-stepped and takes its yield at once).~~ (s78n t20 batch 3) *Steering vector:* the footprint-overlap weight of two units of one formation is not modelled; a non-member Walk unit steers from the segment direction + lane term and passes waypoints by their line only on frames with a non-zero vector ~~(else the port's straight-at-the-waypoint walk)~~ (s78n t26.4: the move step C on every frame); the jetpack caller is not modelled. *Container walk:* the coast after a new order starts at the order (retail: when the formation plan resets the stack); ~~corner fractions and the brake-ahead are no-ops; the start rule is the port's (Close_Enough_Angle);~~ (s78n t26.2: the corner cap, the brake-ahead and start rule A are retail's); Stop also ends the garrison-exit host ([M] in the spec). *Gates:* a raised hover unit's z is not raised (only the rise / lower time and the state); landing transports and build pads are no movement units; a hidden container ignores move orders. Vanilla-visible consequence of the hover gate as written (MOVEMENT §5.3 [H]): the FoC hover types `Lancet_Air_Artillery` and `Gallofree_HTT_Transport` (Hover_Height 80, Walk locomotor) are raised from their first move on, so they take no MovingVsStopped record against grounded standing units and no MovingVsMoving record with grounded movers (`MAL_Rocket_Vehicle`'s Hover_Height 3.0 is commented out in its XML: the MAL does not hover; data test in `alamo_tactical_movement_tests`). The gates and "hidden" are host-fed and not saved: `TacticalBattle::feed_pair_gates` sets them before the movement tick, after every batch of garrison events, at the tick end and after a load (review: a command between a load and the next tick meets the same gates as in the saving run). *Garrison:* the members keep their own garrison records (the group is a host callback) and the squad's radius comes from `TacticalMode::bounds_of` (the model collision bound, the extent box without model data); the move-finished path enters inside 60.0 without 0x443700's 2-D test and corrective move; single units keep the older 2-D reach rule; the exit point is the host centre; `squad_joint_slots` (fog footprint, Bribe's E_T) still reads the steering members only, so it is 0 while the squad is inside (`team_joint_box` keeps the absent members' slots); an absent member is not in the straggler cap and the layouts place steering members only. `MovementStats::push_failures` now also counts blockers that cannot walk. **Review fixes (2026-11-10).** A TEAM entry that goes without a steering member leaving (a suspended member removed, a garrisoned member dying inside, a refused rejoin) is dropped from the entry list at once (`team_ix_rebuild` in `remove_unit` / `rejoin_land_team`): the joint box, the container's radius and the rate no longer differ between the saving run and a loaded copy. `slave_speed` takes the squad's rate from `team_rate` (the pure function of the entries) instead of `Unit::team_accel`, the walk's unsaved copy of the tick before (a loaded battle had 0 there in its first tick: found by the Speed Slave save test). The side steps read the other units' height from the start-of-tick snapshot (`SteerPos`). Decision step 10 stamps only the obstacle it decides ([port] the list has one stamp: older entries that step 3 no longer accepts are dropped before the re-stamp). `order_garrison` refuses a hostile host (`GarrisonSystem::enter_allowed`) before the queues are cleared and the units are sent walking. **Effect** (FoC land skirmish, 30000 ticks, seeds 1-6): all six end with a Rebel win at 15272 / 11734 / 13857 / 20203 / 14665 / 15810 (before: seeds 2, 3, 5 only); no container waits 30 frames or more for a straggler (longest 29); collision holds of 30 frames or more 3 / 1 / 1 / 3 / 2 / 2 per seed, one of 1581 frames in seed 3 (Try Again Later against a standing friendly squad on the destination). `alamo_headless_skirmish_ai_foc_land_end` stays on seed 2. the plan gate is re-tested every tick (retail: on formation updates); the 0x16 rules run from the tick after the arrival (the commit loop ends the goal after `tick_deploy`); the Deployed_Walk_Speed cap is a stand-in for the 0x19 speed rule until t26.2; vf +0x1a0 has no reader; in 0x1A from a walk the speed is kept stale (no speed step); a StoppedVsStopped path start in 0x19 sets the Walk state to None (the walk starts on the next tick). **Who is an auto-deployer** (MOVEMENT §3.2.1 "Who is an auto-deployer", s78n t26 RE 3, 89af943e, [H]): Auto_Deploys defaults to true (0x367250 / 0x367100), so every Deploys type but AT_AT_Walker, Veers_AT_AT_Walker and AT_AA_Walker is an auto-deployer (SPMAT, MPTL, MDU, HAV_Juggernaut, Gallofree_HTT, F9TZ, Destroyer_Droid, MAL); The t20 End Move brake stub (`brake_stub`), the commit-loop snap and the s78l t02 arrival-close rule are gone: a halted or order-less moving unit (no order, a stop, a new order not planned yet, a start delay) coasts in End Move along its last displacement. Counters `MovementStats::walk_coasts / walk_restarts / walk_tau_skips / walk_degen_skips`. Save: movement version 27 (`last_dir`, `started`, `prev_pos` after the turn-done byte; the counters). Hash: land-only 0xEB block (last_dir, prev_pos, started) only when non-default; space CRC goldens, replay_golden.crc, skirmish_ai_foc_golden.crc and the galactic AI logs byte-identical. **[port] choices:** (1) the lane term is applied on every record of a unit with an order (a lone unit is the one member of its formation, MOVEMENT §3.1.1 step 4 Move_To; the port's `in_formation` means > 1 member), the jiggle side only on the order path of an `in_formation` member, from cross(sd, jiggle), with no passability guard; (2) the rest length is 2-D (`remaining_length`); (3) `last_dir` comes from start-of-tick snapshots (prev_pos is set at `add_unit`, so the first frame's coast vector is zero); (4) the aligned test of G uses the first leg's heading (no collision vector); (5) Bike shares the Walk step; (6) the pop into the order path runs in the parallel walk (writes only the unit), the record's end (`end_push`) in the same call; (7) pushed records are always started (§5.4.1 "Push": marked started); (8) the coast of a unit with no started path runs through a temporary `LandMotion` (no path, not started); (9) C.8's exact normalise is v / |v|; (10) turn-done is cleared at every index step; (11) d . n == 0 counts as the degenerate skip; (12) no `Walk_Transition` / `UseSecondaryFacing` (no FoC land type sets them). (13) between a land order and its plan (need_path or the start delay) a moving unit walks on along its old order path - retail's stack reset 0x554480 runs only with the path set (MOVEMENT §3.5.2 G, §4.6.2) - and its arrival there is not the new order's; a standing unit does nothing (d18ec890). **Tests:** `alamo_tactical_movement_t26_tests` t26_walk_step (the hand case: s' 6.385 / 3.489589, the line crossed at (8.968668, 1.031332) with 0.880316 left, the τ = 0 skip of waypoint 2 and the coast to (9.835602, 1.184195); the -0.001 line rule; passing by the line, not the point; the point test; the pop into Q, the coast in End Move), t26_end_move (0x15: 4 short arrives where it stands, 6 short restarts; no snap; a new order behind an AT-AT-like unit (Close_Enough_Angle 30), in the host tick order (the coordinator update after the movement tick): one tick on along the old path in 0x13, then not started: End Move coast, 0x15, then the turn in place; three re-plans of a moving vehicle with CloseEnoughAngleForMoveStart 180 keep 0x13 at full speed), t26_coast_save (save inside an End Move coast, the loaded copy hashes equal and goes on identically), the droid's deployed walk stops within one step of the goal; re-derived to the spec: the locomotion corner swing (under the turn radius v / R), the halt coast v^2 / (2a) - v / 2, Try Again Later (decel 0.00615: they meet moving and rest face to face), the forced path / forced destination / bike / single-member / early-arrival / push-search / StoppedVsStopped end positions (within 5 before the last line or the coast v^2 / (2a); the side-step vector moves X off its line), the arrival-turn reorder (CloseEnoughAngleForMoveStart 180: it turns on the move, 30 + 9.36 k for 16 frames, then the lane term), Path Around's resume (facing within 0.5 deg of the lane-term direction), the terrain detour's creep (its first step after the record turns by R and still fails: a second record, no duplicate rule), real maps (3 of 3240 units stopped by a failed TerrainCollision detour after a full-speed swing at a detour corner; the check counts them); `test_arrival_close` now tests 0x15 directly; `alamo_sim_battle_story_tests` S4: the garrison order at frame 20 (from 60 the trooper's turn on the move took the bunker 10 frames before the Underworld reached the post). kGoldenLand 0xb5781f347d113e74, kGoldenMixed 0x751ad0e40bfa6dea (kGoldenSpace unchanged); save rejection of 26. Goldens regenerated: skirmish_ai_foc_land_golden.crc, skirmish_ai_foc_land_campaign_golden.crc, game_smoke_land.crc (each first change tick 1..64); benchmark scenarios remade for the save bump. Fix d18ec890 (8d09f49f): skirmish_ai_foc_land_campaign_golden.crc (first change ticks 705..768) and the land_battle benchmark regenerated; skirmish_ai_foc_land_golden.crc and game_smoke_land.crc unchanged by it. **Fixed (d18ec890, blocker of the first t26.4 head 72bb12fb):** `alamo_headless_skirmish_ai_foc_land_campaign_outcome_seed6` ran to its 14400-frame cap: the coast for a unit with no started path also caught a moving unit whose new order was not planned yet (need_path until the composition at the next coordinator update; an AI order composes one tick later, MOVEMENT §4.6.2), so every chase re-plan braked the unit to a stop and restarted it (T4B chasers 855 / 859 at 0.5 - 1.1 of 1.092; End Move frames with an order before frame 4800 937 -> 117 after the fix). The fight then went the other way (the Rebels kept two T4B; the Empire's last unit, AT-AT 809, walked a 7216-long Clear_Infantry path at its full 0.78 from frame 5234 and the Rebel AI neither met it nor retreated); with the fix seed 6 ends by the Rebel retreat at 4810 (t26.3: 4087) and campaign seeds 1 - 12 end at 3544 / 5155 / 4249 / 11914 / 2071 / 4810 / 3898 / 8905 / 1690 / 3547 / 4273 / 2443. **Liveness** (FoC land, _MP_LAND_UTAPAU, seeds 1 - 6, 4 workers, 30000-frame cap) before (t26.3, df42bb8d) -> after: end frames 13856 / 14501 / 15635 / 13887 / 15878 / 13737 -> 15056 / 14734 / 14525 / 14775 / 13970 / 14291 (all 6 end, Rebel victory 14; mean 14582 -> 14559); holds >= 150 frames 0 -> 0 (longest per seed 99 / 76 / 10 / 56 / 27 / 80 -> 98 / 66 / 5 / 67 / 126 / 80); longest cannot-hit 351 (seed 2) -> 26 (seed 2; per seed 5 / 26 / 18 / 14 / 11 / 7); no unit idle >= 300 with an order; TerrainCollision records 6 / 3 / 3 / 4 / 2 / 4 -> 10 / 12 / 2 / 7 / 3 / 9; End Move unit-frames 9723 / 13469 / 9193 / 13401 / 14721 / 8541 -> 15258 / 22351 / 11422 / 21435 / 17866 / 15579; new counters: coast frames 1090 / 1964 / 616 / 1431 / 692 / 1226, 0x15 restarts 515 / 1281 / 358 / 792 / 367 / 628, τ = 0 skips 0, degenerate skips 0, mean stop distance from the goal at arrival 2.97 / 0.96 / 0.78 / 1.28 / 1.05 / 1.25 (174 / 155 / 78 / 143 / 99 / 151 arrivals), start-turn ticks 327 / 160 / 13 / 100 / 29 / 204. After the fix (d18ec890; MSVC and clang 245 / 245, 0 warnings): end frames 12987 / 15735 / 14305 / 13850 / 15330 / 14437 (all 6 end, Rebel victory 14; mean 14441); holds >= 150 frames 0 (longest 65 / 99 / 108 / 82 / 75 / 103); longest cannot-hit 31 (seed 3; per seed 5 / 4 / 31 / 30 / 30 / 29); no unit idle >= 300 with an order; TerrainCollision records 9 / 8 / 2 / 13 / 6 / 3; End Move unit-frames 10774 / 13964 / 8169 / 10366 / 14978 / 12045; coast frames 566 / 1044 / 103 / 794 / 345 / 680; 0x15 restarts 50 / 83 / 64 / 111 / 72 / 20; τ = 0 and degenerate skips 0; mean stop distance 2.46 / 2.88 / 0.86 / 1.53 / 1.65 / 1.98 (168 / 193 / 77 / 231 / 137 / 167 arrivals); start-turn ticks 104 / 241 / 0 / 178 / 80 / 86.

- ⬜ **OPEN** · s78n t12 (2026-11-10, spec `docs/re/HERO_CLASH.md` §8.4.1 - §8.4.3): (marked "s78n t12" in code) the incognito bit (+0x3a1 & 0x10) in retail order. **Take_Damage** (`CombatSystem::take_damage_inner` / `damage_gates`): the gates +0x37d invulnerable (kinds other than 0 / 13), `Immune_To_Damage` and a new one, a garrisoned victim (`kUnitGarrisoned`: no damage of any kind), stop a hit before any clear; then the victim's bit goes for any non-projectile source (no weapon / target test) or a projectile whose target is this victim (`DamageRequest::projectile_target`, filled by the direct hit, area damage, the thrown-saber area loop and the remote-bomb detonation, kept through the delayed-damage queue: hashed, saved); then × TAKE_DAMAGE; then the source - a projectile's shooter that resolves loses its bit and gets CAUSE_DAMAGE always, a non-projectile source only when it has the bit (so a self hit gets none; `kUnitSkipTargeting` no longer earns CAUSE_DAMAGE); the damage-enabled gate now sits after the clears and the multipliers. **Blast** sets `kUnitIncognito` on the user at the apply and never clears it (both port clears removed), so `kUnitSkipTargeting` marks only the port's dummy targets and a Blast user is no longer hidden from the port-only `kUnitSkipTargeting` filters. **Vehicle thief**: the ability no longer kills the victim; the host (`tactical_battle_oi_abilities.cpp`, CaptureVariant) spawns the clone, kills the victim only after a successful spawn (no death clone, credited to the thief) and boards the clone, or - a victim type without `Vehicle_Thief_Inside_Clone` (mods only: all 20 shipped targets resolve one) - boards the original vehicle (owner change, `kUnitStolenVehicle | kUnitIncognito`, `flags_3a2 |= 8`); EJECT_VEHICLE_THIEF goes on only when the boarded object has unit ability 46; CAPTURE_VEHICLE on in every case. Combat save 14 → 15 (the delayed-damage record gains the projectile target; only the current version is read). [port] choices: a hit on a victim that is dead but still in the table runs only the gates and the two clears (no redirect, multiplier or stamp); STIM_PACK's self damage passes the user as its source, as retail does (0x3a9e30(V, 1, x, V): its incognito bit goes, no CAUSE_DAMAGE, its own Damage list and its owner's AI damage multiplier scale it), but a self hit keeps no kill credit and no damage-record source - retail's handling of those is not in the spec, so the final-blow player, the story's killer and DAMAGE_TRACKING stay as for a sourceless hit; the victim is removed in the host pass of the same tick, after the spawn (retail: inside the apply), by the world removal 0x391310 without Die (`CombatSystem::remove_from_world` (named `remove_unit(id, killer, frame)` until s78n t19): no hardpoint dies - so no HardpointEvent is left for the story feed after that tick's hardpoint pass - and there is no death clone, death projectile, DeathBehavior draw, Death_Explosions particle or death clip; Tactical_Respawn / Destruction_Survivors and the DeathEvent, i.e. the story's Destroyed, the victory count and the loss record, stay), processed there like a script kill, so it comes after that tick's other deaths; a victim that died between the apply and the host pass - with a clone the clone is still spawned and boarded, without one nothing is boarded and CAPTURE_VEHICLE stays on; a thief that died in that window does not take over an original, and with a clone type the clone is still spawned and the victim removed but the clone stays an ordinary vehicle of the victim's owner (no ability 46, no owner change, no stolen / incognito flags, nobody boards); a boarded object that is not a garrison holder (retail creates the container pack on demand, 0x390740; 17 of the 18 shipped `*_Captured` clones have no garrison behaviour) becomes a `HolderKind::VehicleThief` holder (`engine/tactical/garrison`, garrison save 1 → 2): not a garrison - `order_enter` / `can_garrison` refuse it, an approach to it is dropped, it has no free slot - the thief does not leave by a leave / eject-garrison order and stays through an owner change of the vehicle, and when the vehicle dies (the eject ability's Take_Damage kind 13, `docs/re/REMAINING_GAPS.md` §5.1) or is removed the thief is released alive at the vehicle's position, a mover again; a thief whose type has no GARRISON_UNIT is registered at the boarding as a board-only occupant (`kBoardOnlyValue`: never taken by a garrison order); deaths raised between ticks by command-phase ability effects (the eject's vehicle kill, a STIM_PACK that kills its user) are processed at the start of the next battle tick - before, the host never saw them - but their hardpoint / survivor events and the story feed of such a death are still lost with the per-tick lists the combat tick clears; a host killed between ticks (script damage) releases its occupants where they sit instead of at the map origin; a loading battle creates the garrison / capture systems when the saved garrison part has them and its own rebuilt setup made no garrison use (the first use came mid-battle: a reinforcement, a theft); a failed clone spawn (retail: the apply fails, no CAPTURE_VEHICLE) is unreachable here and leaves CAPTURE_VEHICLE on.

No save change (the hook and `clr_types_` are derived state).

- ⬜ **OPEN** · cu8f t04 (2026-11-07, `docs/re/CU8F_T04_T06_RE.md` §1 / §2): [port] choices (marked "cu8f t04a" / "cu8f t04b" in code). **Targeting stickiness:** `CombatSystem` `Unit::stickiness` = TD +0x158 (spawn 0.0; a negative value reads the type's value at every test); saved (combat save v13; only the current version is read, cu6) and hashed in `hash_extended` (tag 0x58) only when non-zero. A TEAM owner is a container that is itself a combat unit (live units carry its id as `team`) or, on land, the team_leader member standing in for a container that is not a combat unit; its "first member" is the team's first live unit in ascending id (the original's team-list order is not modelled). The members of a store TEAM object (Lua setters, task-force release) are written in store order. Every combat unit counts as having DAMAGE_TRACKING (the 5 s `damage_rate` window). Destroyed destroyable hard points stay in the sum at max(0, health) = 0, so a target whose destroyable hard points are all gone has time to kill 0 and is sticky even at threshold 0. A container target exists only when the container is itself a combat unit; the app's TacticalBattle containers are not, so the team sum is dormant there. The team member list is derived (rebuilt in phase A and on read, not saved). Lua `Set_Targeting_Stickiness_Time_Threshold` writes the battle `CombatSystem` through `ObjectBridge::abilities` (the `RemainingScriptHooks::set_stickiness` / `set_prefer_ground` hooks were removed). **Prefer ground over space:** the flag is kept by `GalacticSim` (the ids set to false; saved, galactic save v5; only the current version is read; hashed in the "OIG1" tail, "PGOS", once a live unit is false). The Lua setter writes it only for objects of the player world's galactic store; a tactical object keeps only the binding's record (tactical code never reads it). The galactic AI's auto-land (`auto_land_free_units`) skips a unit whose flag is false. **cu8g t04 (auto-land to §2.2):** the gate runs in the spec's order (AI player; `unit_safe` and not `unit_in_transit`; TRANSPORT and in a fleet; prefer ground; the orbited planet below Max_Ground_Forces_On_Planet (`surface_forces`) with an accessible surface), then FleetManagement op 2 (detach into a fleet of its own) and op 5 (land), both logged ("auto-land detach <id>", "auto-land <id>"). [port] "Has TRANSPORT" = a ground unit whose travel type (the Company_Transport_Unit, else its own type) has the TRANSPORT behaviour (the sim carries the company in the fleet where the original carries its transport; every vanilla Company_Transport_Unit has TRANSPORT); a TRANSPORT unit that is not a ground unit (hero ship) is not a candidate, the port's op 5 would refuse it. "Fleet not moving" is the transit test; "not in battle" is dropped (not in the spec). Kept as a port limit: the planet must be the player's own (the port's op 5 lands only there, no invasion through op 5; the original's outcome at a foreign planet is not modelled, so nothing, no detach either, is built there). Nothing is built while the galaxy is paused (both ops refused). Is_Unit_Safe's body (0x6902b0 calls 0x375220, 0x3f9530, 0x39a2c0, 0x397120 per the call graph) is still the [L] "hostile fleet in orbit" reading.
